# SAIRN Active Work — Fourth

Per-session active-work log for the **Fourth** session (`Documents\SAIRN-fourth`).
Split out of the shared `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md` on 2026-08-24 because four
concurrent sessions appending to one file produced repeated merge conflicts
in a single night. Each session now appends only to its own file, so two
sessions can never collide on the same lines.

**Append your entries here, not to `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.**

The four sessions and their files — one row each, all equal, no special case:

| Session | Clone | File |
|---|---|---|
| Hank | `Documents\SAIRN-hank` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md` |
| CC | `Documents\SAIRN-cc` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cc.md` |
| Cody | `Documents\SAIRN-cody` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md` |
| Fourth | `Documents\SAIRN-fourth` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-fourth.md` ← this file |

Still read the other three (`SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cc.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md`) before starting anything —
the point of the shared file was to avoid two sessions touching the same app
file at once, and that reason has not gone away. Splitting the file removes
the write collision, not the need to check.

One line per task: app/file(s) touched, a one-line task description,
timestamp. Add a line before starting anything new.

This file starts empty: the Fourth clone had no logged entries in the shared
file at split time (2026-08-24), so there was nothing to move. Cross-session
context that predates the split — including the `cc-work` worktree
correction — stays in `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.

- sql/introspect_undeclared_tables_2026-08-26.sql (NEW), docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- MIGRATIONS FOR THE THREE UNDECLARED TABLES ARE BLOCKED ON REAL SCHEMA, AND I DID NOT GUESS THEM. Michael asked for migration files for business_profiles, ai_memories and employees matching the live schema exactly, pulling structure from information_schema first. I have NO information_schema access -- established last turn and unchanged: no psql, no supabase CLI, no pg driver, and no connection string of any kind in the Vercel project (only SUPABASE_URL/ANON_KEY/SERVICE_ROLE_KEY). So the correct move was to write the introspection query, not the migrations. WHAT I DID INSTEAD OF GUESSING: read every call site and recorded the code-side expectations IN the query file as an explicit CROSS-CHECK, not as the answer -- business_profiles license_hash/app_id/data/shop_id/updated_at with UNIQUE(license_hash,app_id) proven by ?on_conflict= at api/sd-data.js:288; employees customer_email/employee_id/source_app/status/data/updated_at with UNIQUE(customer_email,employee_id) at :432; ai_memories license_hash/app_id/shop_id/data/created_at, plain INSERT with no on_conflict so a surrogate key is likely and created_at must default server-side (writer never sends it, reader orders by it, :303/:320). The file says in terms that if the DB disagrees, the DB wins and the disagreement gets its own index line. Code shows COLUMNS; it cannot show types, defaults, PKs, indexes, RLS policies or grants, and business_profiles backs every StoneDesk profile read/write, so a near-miss there is worse than the gap. QUERY: six independent read-only SELECTs, no shared state, run one at a time -- columns, constraints via pg_get_constraintdef() so the text is character-identical rather than reconstructed, indexes, RLS + every policy in full, grants, and a sanity section naming all three so a PARTIAL answer is visible instead of silent. Section 6 is to be run FIRST. Mechanically verified read-only: 0 insert/update/delete/drop/create/grant/revoke/truncate/alter outside comments, 6 selects, 6 statements. TWO THINGS FLAGGED FOR DECISION RATHER THAN BURIED: employees is scoped by customer_email, NOT license_hash -- the only table on the platform keyed that way, and a migration would enshrine it. And section 5 doubles as a grant check: expect select/insert/update and NO delete; if DELETE appears, these three were missed by the 2026-08-25 sweep exactly as the two execute format(...) loops were, and tonight's durability claim needs revisiting. File named 2026-08-26 rather than -25 because that is when it was actually written; the finding it addresses is from the 25th and the header says so. 2026-08-26
- sql/business_profiles_schema.sql, sql/ai_memories_schema.sql, sql/employees_schema.sql (all NEW), docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THREE UNDECLARED LIVE TABLES NOW DECLARED, written from the real introspected structure rather than from the code. Michael ran all six sections of introspect_undeclared_tables_2026-08-26.sql and returned the full structure; every column, nullability, default, constraint and index in the three files matches what came back, verified column-by-column by script rather than by eye. Declared tables 224 -> 227, live_but_not_declared 25 -> 22. THE CODE-SIDE PREDICTIONS HELD ON SHAPE AND FAILED ON SURFACE, which is the whole justification for not writing these from the code: both UNIQUE constraints were correctly derived from ?on_conflict= (business_profiles license_hash/app_id at :288, employees customer_email/employee_id at :432), and the ai_memories call -- surrogate key likely, created_at must default server-side -- was right. But nothing in the code could see id, created_at, the defaults, or the 64KB CHECK on all three. Two nullable columns would have been easy to get wrong in the other direction and are correct here: ai_memories.shop_id (writer stamps it inside a try/catch commented 'non-fatal -- memory still saves unlinked') and employees.status (defaulted in JS at :423, not in the column). GRANT CHECK CAME BACK CLEAN: service_role holds INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE and NO DELETE on all three, so they were NOT missed by the 2026-08-25 sweep and that durability claim stands. TWO FINDINGS DISCLOSED IN THE HEADERS, NOT SILENTLY FIXED, both now their own index rows because both are design decisions above a migration file's pay grade. (1) ai_memories has NO unique constraint -- primary key on id and nothing else, and the code does not want one: plain INSERT with no on_conflict, read is order=created_at.desc&limit=10, live index idx_mem_scope(license_hash, created_at DESC). Append-only is the actual behaviour; the open question is whether that is intended, since the NAME suggests a per-licence record. Adding a constraint would change behaviour and could fail on existing duplicates, so the file adds none and says why. (2) anon and authenticated still hold TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER on all three -- the default-ACL baseline removed everywhere else, which never covered these because a sweep driven off sql/ inherits sql/'s omissions. Same blind spot one level down. Each file carries the revoke-anon/authenticated line as the house idiom so a re-provision does not inherit it, but every header flags it as the ONE non-inert statement in the file. employees is the one to weigh first: ten apps share that roster. Worth checking the other 22 live_but_not_declared tables for the same baseline. 2026-08-26

Index row now owned by Michael as a product decision, not left unassigned. 22-TABLE ACL CHECK WRITTEN, NOT ASSUMED: sql/undeclared_table_acl_check_2026-08-26.sql, read-only (4 statements, 7 selects, zero write verbs, 681 VALUES entries = 227 x 3). - docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- MY HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG AND THE CONTROL CAUGHT IT. I predicted the anon/authenticated TRUNCATE baseline would sit only on live-but-not-declared tables (sweep driven off sql/ inherits sql/'s omissions). Section 3 -- the control over DECLARED tables, written specifically so the prediction could be falsified -- came back dirty too: 158 tables each for anon and authenticated, 316 grant-rows, including heavily-used declared tables like dnt_appointments and grd_jobs. Declaration status is irrelevant. REAL ROOT CAUSE: every grant-hardening pass tonight targeted service_role ONLY. Verified at append_only_grant_audit.sql:192-193, which reads `revoke truncate, references, trigger, maintain on tables from service_role` and names no other role. The three tables were never a special case. Writing the control in is what made this findable; had I only run section 2 I would have 'confirmed' a wrong mechanism with real data. SEVERITY ASSESSED, and the first question came back the OPPOSITE of what the proxy-only model predicted. anon is NOT unused: browsers ship a publishable key (sb_publishable_... at stonedesk.html:24668, :34319, sairnbiz.html:835) and hit intake_submissions directly with select/update/DELETE (stonedesk.html:31984, :32119, :32130) -- already recorded at full_crud_truncate_sweep_2026-08-24.sql:351,502. But the excess verbs have no invocation path: PostgREST exposes no TRUNCATE and no DDL verb, there are ZERO .rpc() calls in any browser file, and all five SQL functions are SECURITY INVOKER with revoke-all-from-public + grant-execute-to-service_role only. Two precision points that change how this should be read: RLS is NOT a control (TRUNCATE bypasses row-level security entirely, so the svc-only policies do nothing against it), and a publishable key is public by design, so whatever anon holds is effectively held by anyone. VERDICT: latent not active, Medium -- unreachable because no verb exposes it, not because anything guards it; High the moment any SECURITY DEFINER function is granted to anon/public or any RPC runs dynamic SQL. SCOPE PROPOSED, NOTHING WRITTEN YET, awaiting Michael: roles anon+authenticated (verify separately whether authenticated has ANY legitimate use -- no code path signs a user into Supabase auth); verbs TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER ONLY, explicitly not S/I/U/D since anon legitimately needs three of those on intake_submissions; NAMED CARVE-OUT and the sweep's sharpest risk -- intake_submissions must keep anon's SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE or StoneDesk's intake panel breaks silently, asserted BEFORE and after; fix the SOURCE too by extending ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES to both roles or every new table re-acquires the baseline; same shape as the two sweeps that worked (R6 -> R4 -> Section 0 baseline -> 1 discover -> 2 revoke commented -> 3 verify -> 4 cleanup); verification INVERTED as in the DELETE sweep since LOST is the objective -- assert the expected LOST rows all in {TRUNCATE,REFERENCES,TRIGGER}, zero GAINED, zero LOST of any other verb, table count unchanged, carve-out intact. 2026-08-26

Six-part shape matching the two service_role sweeps: R6 owner gate -> R4 pre-flight -> Section 0 baseline (+0b carve-out asserted BEFORE) -> Section 1 list-free discover (+1b what is NOT touched) -> Section 2 revoke, commented -> Section 3 3a/3b/3c/3d -> Section 4 cleanup, commented. Stage A revokes ALL on tables, sequences and routines, plus ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES widened past tables-only. All list-free (`all tables/sequences/routines in schema public` resolves from the catalog at execution time, so it cannot drift from an enumerated list or miss the live-but-undeclared tables that started this thread). Stage B (revoke usage on schema public) is present ONLY as a commented, explicitly-labelled do-not-uncomment-here block, so a future reader cannot mistake it for part of this run. WHY 'revoke all' ON TABLES IS NOT A WIDER BLAST RADIUS THAN THE THREE-VERB DRAFT, stated in the file because it reads backwards: 1b came back EMPTY, so TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER are all either role holds anywhere. Both statements reach the same end state -- zero. The wider verb list is the same change, stated so it stays correct if someone grants one of these roles something before it runs. TWO ADDITIONS THE THREE-VERB DRAFT DID NOT NEED. Section 0c: a baseline for SEQUENCES and ROUTINES, because Section 0 captured role_table_grants only and a revoke you cannot diff is a revoke you cannot verify -- sequences read via aclexplode on pg_class rather than an information_schema view, since sequence privileges are USAGE/SELECT/UPDATE and no single IS view reports all three cleanly per grantee. Section 3e: the matching verification, expecting zero rows; without it the sequence and routine statements would be unverified and would still read as 'swept'. Also added 3f, which records schema-USAGE state as Stage B's ONLY before-reading, since Stage B has no baseline of its own -- and Section 4 now says keep BOTH baseline tables until Stage B has run too, not just Stage A. ONE STATEMENT I FLAGGED RATHER THAN SLIPPED IN: `revoke all on all routines in schema public` also touches EXTENSION functions, because public is not only SAIRN's schema -- gen_random_uuid(), which all three migrations written tonight use as a column default, lives there via pgcrypto. Safe, and the file gives the reasoning not just the verdict: revoking EXECUTE from anon/authenticated does not touch service_role, which performs every insert on this platform, so those defaults keep working; the five SAIRN functions already revoke all from public and grant execute to service_role only. Known future cost named in the file: if anon is ever legitimately granted INSERT on a table whose default calls a public function, it also needs EXECUTE on that function -- one extra GRANT then, not a surprise. Verified after writing: the ONLY executable statements in the whole file are the two baseline-table creates. Every revoke and every ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES is commented. INTAKE ROW RESTRUCTURED per Michael -- same row, not a new one, since both symptoms trace to the one gap. The customer-facing 404 is now symptom (1) and explicitly labelled the urgent half: it leaves the building, reaching a paying customer's customer, and the false 'Intake link copied!' toast means the user has no reason to check. The silent read fallback is (2). Caught my own formatting break in the process: the restructure introduced real newlines into a markdown table row, splitting it into three physical lines -- found by the column-count check, not by eye, and rejoined with <br>. 2026-08-26

PRE-CHECKS: 0c zero rows (sequence and routine statements are no-ops -- worth KNOWING rather than assuming, which is exactly why 0c was added rather than skipped), 1b zero rows again, Section 1 now 160 tables each. Michael's results: 3a empty, 3b six rows all LOST zero GAINED, 3c remaining_rows 0, 3d postgres's default ACL clean for both roles, 3e empty, 3f recorded (both roles still hold USAGE on public -- Stage B's target, expected). THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE SAME BUG CLASS ONE DOOR OVER, NOW THREE DEEP: supabase_admin has its OWN default ACL in public granting anon/authenticated arwdDxtm on tables -- full CRUD, materially WORSE than the three verbs Stage A removed -- plus rwU on sequences and X on functions. append_only_grant_audit.sql:192-193 fixed the default ACL for postgres and named only service_role, which is how anon/authenticated survived on 159 tables. Stage A fixed it for both roles and named only postgres. Each fix was correct and each was scoped to exactly what its author had looked at. It surfaced ONLY because 3d prints the whole default-ACL picture instead of asserting the one thing that changed -- that verification style is what caught it and is worth keeping. PROBABLY INERT, AND I DID NOT CLOSE IT ON THAT. R6 printed current_user=postgres, so the SQL editor -- the only path any migration here has ever taken -- runs as postgres, and a default ACL only fires for objects created BY the role it names. But that is a claim about how one person runs migrations through one UI today, not a property of the database; Supabase's own machinery (extension installs/upgrades, dashboard tooling, managed features) does act as supabase_admin and public is not off-limits to it -- pgcrypto's gen_random_uuid() already lives there. Failure would be silent and delayed: no error, a table simply arrives with anon holding full CRUD. WROTE THE CHECK RATHER THAN THE FIX: read-only, 6 selects, zero write verbs. Section 1 settles it with OWNERSHIP EVIDENCE rather than reasoning (100% postgres = strong evidence the entry never fires; any supabase_admin-owned object = proof it does), 1b covers functions separately where an extension is likeliest to have left something, 2 names any exposure, 4 reprints the full picture. SECTION 3 IS THE ONE TO READ FIRST because it may make the fix moot: ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE supabase_admin requires membership in that role, postgres is not a superuser on Supabase, and if is_member is false the statement cannot run from the SQL editor at all -- the real options then become a support request or a deliberate accept-and-monitor. Better to know that before writing a statement that cannot execute. ALSO FLAGGED, NOT LET SLIDE: the count-drift prediction (158+2=160, must fall by exactly 2 after Section 4) is STILL UNTESTED, because Section 4 is on hold until Stage B. Not writing it up as confirmed. STAGE B PROPOSED with its own window: one statement, rollback line in the clipboard BEFORE the revoke rather than looked up after, and post-checks in order -- 3f expect zero, then a real api/sd-data.js read (service_role path, must be UNAFFECTED, a failure there means roll back immediately), then the Supabase dashboard table view, which is the part no query can predict and the whole reason this statement does not share a run. SEQUENCING RECOMMENDATION: run the supabase_admin check FIRST -- independent of Stage B, but if it comes back live then a second granting role is handing anon full CRUD on new tables, and closing schema USAGE while that door is open is the smaller of the two jobs. 2026-08-26

Tier 2 therefore carries only what this pass touched and could not confirm, and says so in the document. TWO SOURCES DEFEATED THE METHOD AND ONE NOTE CHANGED ITS REASON: mass.gov re-tested per the never-record-blocked-without-retesting rule and returned 403 today, NOT the landing shell recorded on 2026-08-26 -- different obstacle, different remedy (find a non-mass.gov route, do not re-fetch); Texas SOS retired texreg.sos.state.tx.us to an Appian portal that renders via JS and returns no regulatory text. ONE CONFLICT LEFT OPEN RATHER THAN RESOLVED BY PREFERENCE: the CA 1796.19 fetch SUMMARISED the administering body as Health Care Services while CDSS's own CCLD page puts home care under CDSS; the statute says only "the department", so neither is cited. That one is logged as a method note too -- where a fetch summarises instead of quoting, the summary is the fetch layer inferring, not the source speaking. CA METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING: codes_displaySection.xhtml returns a "multiple results exist" stub for any section amended twice in a session (1796.44 was repealed AND re-added by the same chapter); codes_displayText.xhtml at article level returned the full text. The stub is itself a signal that a cached secondary summary is stale. NOT CLOSED: 46 states unattempted, WA continuing-education hours not in the section read (the commonly-quoted 12 is NOT confirmed and is not carried), NY 40/75 not fetched, state domestic-worker overtime not started. 2026-08-29
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-29-sairnsenior-domestic-worker-overtime.md (NEW), docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-29-sairnsenior-state-variation.md (Fourth) -- THE OPEN ITEM'S OWN PREMISE WAS WRONG, AND THAT IS THE FINDING. Item 5 said state domestic-worker overtime rules "a federal FLSA rescission would not displace". Two of six states read say otherwise: Connecticut C.G.S. 31-58(e) excludes domestic service in a private home "except any individual in domestic service employment as defined in the regulations of the federal Fair Labor Standards Act", and Oregon ORS 653.547(1)(b)(B)(ix) excludes "individuals performing companionship services exempt from the provisions of the Fair Labor Standards Act". Coverage in both is a federal question wearing a state citation, so DOL's RIN 1235-AA51 (projected November 2026) propagates straight into them. Nobody goes looking for that while the note says state rules are insulated. THREE CATEGORIES NOW, NOT TWO: independent (CA/NY/IL), federally coupled (CT/OR), and agency-carved-out -- ORS 653.547(1)(b)(B)(vii) excludes "individuals employed by organizations licensed as required by ORS 443.015 or 443.315", i.e. Oregon's Act does not reach in-home care agency employees at all, which is exactly the worker SAIRNsenior schedules. CALIFORNIA IS THE MIRROR IMAGE and the contrast is the point: Lab. Code 1451 defines personal attendant as employed "by a private householder or third-party employer", so agency caregivers ARE covered, subject to an 80%-of-weekly-hours test, with IHSS providers excluded outright. TIER 1 THRESHOLDS, all primary: CA 9/day or 45/week (Lab. Code 1454); NY 40, 44 live-in (Lab. Law 170); OR 40, 44 live-in (653.547(2)(a)); IL 40 (820 ILCS 105/4a, with 105/3(d)'s "notwithstanding" clause overriding the fewer-than-four-employees exclusion); MA 40 by ABSENCE -- c.151 s1A's twenty exemptions include no domestic service, and c.149 s190 routes the rate back to c.151 rather than setting one, so reading s190 alone looks like there is no overtime rule; NV 40 or 8/day, live-in EXEMPTIBLE BY WRITTEN AGREEMENT (NRS 608.018(3)(p)) -- a rule that depends on a stored document, not on hours. NV 608.0195 FOUND WHILE CHECKING SOMETHING ELSE and is the sharpest scheduling-to-payroll rule in the pass: up to 8 hours of scheduled sleep excludable by written agreement for HOME CARE WORKERS specifically, any interruption counts as work, and if interruptions drop the period below 5 hours the ENTIRE period is compensable. WA has no blanket domestic exclusion -- 49.46.010(3)(b)'s casual-labor exclusion ends "unless performed in the course of the employer's trade, business, or profession", which puts agency-dispatched care back in, and (3)(j) now carves back domestic workers expressly. BOTH DEAD PUBLISHERS CLEARED, AND I CORRECTED MY OWN MORNING DIAGNOSIS. I had written that the MA obstacle was a bot wall "not an empty page", narrowing the prior pass's landing-shell note. Both are true simultaneously: mass.gov 403s WebFetch AND curl with a browser UA (14,061 bytes of HTML, not a PDF), while Chrome renders it fine -- and the page IS a shell whose only content is a PDF link. The prior note was right; my correction was the wrong shape. The actual unblock is that malegislature.gov is a DIFFERENT HOST and was never blocked: every MA statute cited today came from it first try. Massachusetts is not a blocked state, mass.gov is a blocked host, and only the CMRs live there. Got 105 CMR 155.000's authority (M.G.L. c.111 ss72F-72L), scope (all individuals working in or employed by a facility, home health agency, homemaker agency or hospice program) and section list; ss155.010 and 155.016 text still unread -- the PDF renders canvas-only and resisted screenshot paging. TEXAS: the Appian replacement for texreg NEVER REACHES DOCUMENT-IDLE, so get_page_text, find and screenshot all time out against it and the rule= URL param is silently dropped -- three attempts, recorded unusable rather than retried. Chapter structure was readable (26 TAC ch.558, subchapters A-H, Subchapter G "Home Health Aides" holding a single rule 558.701); the requirements themselves came from HHSC's OWN pre-survey training modules on apps.hhs.texas.gov: 75 hours minimum, at least 16 classroom before clinical, at least 16 clinical, competency evaluation by an RN, cited there to 558.701(e)-(f) and 558.401(f)/558.701(c). LABELLED AS AGENCY-RESTATING-ITS-OWN-RULE, one step removed from the codified text -- plan against it, do not quote it to a customer as the rule. Personal Assistance Services (the unlicensed personal assistant, the larger half of the Texas market) remains entirely unretrieved. METHOD NOTE THAT SAVED TWO STATES: when a fetch summariser says a chapter page "does not contain" a section, it usually means the page was TRUNCATED, not that the text is absent -- cga.ct.gov chap_558 and oregonlegislature ORS 653 both came back "not present" twice and both contained the sections in full; curl + local extraction found each on the first try. And both the CT and OR findings sit in DEFINITION sections, not overtime sections, so searching for the operative rule would have missed both. NOT DONE: NJ (justia 403, no official host tried), Seattle/municipal ordinances, 44 states. 2026-08-29
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-29-sairnsenior-evv-guidance-and-state-round-2.md (NEW), api/_lib/sen-evv-readiness.js (Fourth) -- CMS EVV GUIDANCE PAGE LOCATED, AND BOTH PRIOR EXPLANATIONS FOR WHY IT COULD NOT BE WERE HALF RIGHT. New URL: medicaid.gov/medicaid/home-community-based-services/home-community-based-services-guidance-additional-resources/electronic-visit-verification, reached by letting a real browser follow the redirect off the old .../guidance/electronic-visit-verification-evv path. The pre-2026-08-28 note said medicaid.gov and cms.gov 403 automated fetch; the 2026-08-28 correction said BOTH return 200 and the page 404s because it moved. Measured today: EVERY medicaid.gov URL tried, including the site ROOT, returns 403 to WebFetch AND to curl with a full Chrome UA, while cms.gov returns 200 and Chrome loads all of them. So the host does block automated clients AND the page had moved -- each note held one half, and each half prescribes a remedy that fails alone ("blocked" says wait, "moved" says go look), when what worked was a different CLIENT. Durable form: a reachability claim is about a host, a client and a date, and drops wrong quietly if any of the three is missing. Captured the page's own deadline/FMAP language verbatim and its TEN-DOCUMENT index (the load-bearing four: May 2018 CIB, May 2018 FAQ, Aug 2019 Additional EVV Guidance CIB, Dec 2022 good-faith-effort exemption guidance). NONE READ -- restated in the module as a READING task, not a search, rather than claimed closed. STATE ROUND 2, and the schema finding is the valuable part: of eleven states now read on aide training, only FOUR state an hour number (WA 75, TX 75, GA 40, CA 5+5) and GA's 40 is only one of four alternative pathways. PA, VA, MN, NC, NY, FL-homemaker and NJ impose COMPETENCY, not hours. A per-state required_training_hours integer would be empty or wrong across most of the map and would invite someone to fill it from a vendor blog; the right shape is a qualification-PATHWAY model where a worker matches one of N enumerated routes, only some hour-denominated. New Tier 1: NY 10 NYCRR 700.2 (HHA approved program or equivalent exam; PCA four routes incl. one full year of agency experience and 18 NYCRR 505.14); PA 28 Pa. Code 611.55 competency + 611.52 criminal history within 1 year preceding application; GA 111-8-65-.09 four PCA routes, fingerprint check under 111-8-12, TB screening per CDC before starting work, bonding where unlimited access to client funds; VA 12VAC5-381-290 six qualification routes incl. 42 CFR 484.36(b) and the DMAS 2003 curriculum, plus -110 State Police report within 30 days of employment and not dated more than 90 days before; MN 144A.4795 two-tier basic vs comprehensive with a practical skills test and no hours; NC 10A NCAC 13J core competencies verified BY AN RN for in-home aides not on the nurse aide registry hired after 2009-04-01; NJ DWBR effective 2024-07-01 (1.5x over 40, live-in minimum 8 hours pay per on-duty day, written agreement over five hours a month, 10-minute paid break every four hours). FLORIDA'S REGISTRATION/LICENSURE SPLIT IS A COMMERCIAL FACT, NOT A DETAIL: 400.509 lets a homemaker/companion organisation REGISTER for $50 per biennium with NO training or in-service hours in the section at all, while 408.809's level 2 screening with 5-year rescreening attaches to LICENSED providers -- two businesses that look identical to a customer, different obligations, and the trigger is whether anything crosses into home health. ONE FAMOUS NUMBER CHECKED AND IT IS NOT WHERE IT IS CITED: NY's 40-hour PCA / 75-hour HHA figures are NOT in 10 NYCRR 700.2, which points to department-approved programs and 18 NYCRR 505.14. Still unverified, now with a reason. CODE CHANGE IS COMMENT-AND-STRING ONLY and was verified as such rather than asserted: node --check passes, module loads with exports unchanged (FEDERAL_ELEMENTS, FEDERAL_SOURCE, CHECKABLE_STATUSES, checkVisit, summarize), and a diff filter for changed lines that are neither comments nor string literals returns EMPTY across 23 insertions / 10 deletions. DISCLOSED: the full 30-check Guardian pass was NOT run (count re-read from the skill's own "## The 30 Checks" heading today, not trusted from CLAUDE.md) because no executable line changed -- a judgment call, written down so it is reviewable rather than invisible. BLOCKED ON ONE SHARED MECHANISM: MA 105 CMR 155.010/.016 and the four CMS PDFs are both stuck behind canvas-backed PDF viewers -- five attempts on the MA file across two sessions (scroll, Page_Down, scrollbar drag, #page=, fresh tab) never left page 1. Solving PDF extraction once unblocks both and is now the highest-leverage tooling gap in this line. METHOD: curl beat WebFetch on four state hosts (regs.health.ny.gov 403s WebFetch and 200s curl; reports.oah.state.nc.us is HTTP-only and WebFetch force-upgrades to HTTPS), and batch status-probing eight publishers before fetching any caught a 410 Gone in seconds. NOT DONE: TX unlicensed personal assistant requirements (only administrator-side found), ~35 states. 2026-08-29
- api/_lib/sen-evv-readiness.js, tests/sairnsenior/test-evv-readiness.js, tools/fetch_blocked_doc.sh (NEW), tools/js_code_only_diff.py (NEW), docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-guardian-pdf-unblock-and-cms-guidance.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- GUARDIAN CLEAN, THE PDF BLOCKER WAS NEVER WHAT I SAID IT WAS, AND MASSACHUSETTS + TWO CMS DOCUMENTS ARE NOW READ. GUARDIAN v2 full pass on sen-evv-readiness.js, count re-read from the skill's own "## The 30 Checks" heading: zero failures, zero blocking failures. Check 6 fired 5 box-char hits (lines 10/24/72/149/294) and ALL are in // comments -- proven by stripping, not eyeballed: zero survive a comment+string strip. 0d checked rather than assumed (callers: api/sd-data.js:40, sairnsenior.html:1165, the test file). Checks 3/4/7/8/9/12-18/20/26/27 N/A for a pure-function lib and are marked N/A with the reason, not silently skipped; 23 retired per the skill. MY 2026-08-29 "comment-and-string-only" CLAIM IS NOW PROVEN, and the method is the part worth keeping: a grep filter for non-comment non-string lines is a regex guessing at JS tokenisation, so I wrote a real tokeniser (tools/js_code_only_diff.py) that strips comments and empties every string/template literal, WITH A SELF-TEST -- the stripped output must still pass node --check, which it will not if the tokeniser mis-reads a regex or string boundary. Both versions parse; code-only sha256 identical (f3a570de...). THE PDF BLOCKER IS SOLVED AND MY OWN DIAGNOSIS OF IT WAS WRONG IN THE EXPENSIVE DIRECTION. I called mass.gov a "request-fingerprint block, not a UA check" on the evidence that curl WITH a browser UA got 403. It is a HEADER-SET check: bare curl fails, UA-only curl fails, curl sending the full browser set (Accept, Accept-Language, Accept-Encoding, sec-ch-ua*, Sec-Fetch-*, Upgrade-Insecure-Requests, Referer) gets 200. mass.gov 105 CMR 155 -> 118,257 bytes; medicaid.gov CIB -> 168,538; FAQ -> 200,988. pypdf was already installed, so extraction is local and NO BROWSER IS NEEDED AT ALL -- the canvas-viewer wall that ate five attempts across two sessions was never on the critical path. "Request fingerprint" implies TLS/IP and points the next reader at browser automation; "header check" is four extra headers. I tested one hypothesis and named a bigger one. Script promoted to tools/fetch_blocked_doc.sh. MASSACHUSETTS CLOSED: 105 CMR 155.002 scope covers home health AND homemaker agencies; 155.010(E)(3) makes contacting the Registry a PRE-HIRE duty for any individual providing direct care or having access to patients or their property, (E)(4) prohibits hiring anyone carrying a finding; 155.010(G)(3) requires CORI on an applicant under FINAL CONSIDERATION for a direct-care post; 155.016 is the registry itself and 155.015 allows petition for removal after one year. THIS ADDS A FOURTH AXIS to the pathways model -- a state-run adverse-findings registry with a pre-hire QUERY duty and a hiring prohibition, which a schema carrying only "training route" and "background check" cannot represent: the query is an event with a date and an outcome and it must precede the hire. CMS GUIDANCE, 2 OF 10 READ (CIB + FAQ, both 2018-05-16), and it carries three things the statute does not. (1) SCOPE CARVE-OUT: CMS interprets "in-home visit" to EXCLUDE PCS in congregate residential settings with 24-hour availability -- group homes, assisted living -- plus PACE. A visit is not in scope just because it is a Medicaid personal care visit. (2) NO FEDERAL GPS REQUIREMENT: capturing where service starts and stops is "sufficient", IVR named as a common alternative; continuous tracking is a state option, never a federal floor. (3) NO UNIFORM SYSTEM, five models named, and the good-faith-effort relief is "a limited exception for the FIRST YEAR", narrower than the landing page implies. CODE CHANGE IS NOT COMMENT-ONLY THIS TIME AND I SAID SO RATHER THAN CARRYING THE PRIOR DESCRIPTION FORWARD BY HABIT: two new residual_gaps entries, so the tokeniser correctly reports CODE CHANGED and the UI gains two bullets. Three tests failed instantly on assertEq(residual_gaps.length, 2) -- the tests working. MADE THE ASSERTIONS STRONGER, NOT LOOSER: an exact count turns "we disclosed more" into a failure, which is pressure in exactly the wrong direction for a module whose purpose is disclosure. Now length >= 4 plus a NAMED assertion per gap, so adding a fifth passes and deleting any existing one fails -- the count could not catch a delete-plus-add, this can. In the roll-up test the count became a comparison against the per-visit result, since that test's subject is object identity. 30 passed / 0 failed after. Diff shape checked before commit per the Guardian lesson: 52/24 and 17/4, nothing swept in. SCOPE CARVE-OUT DISCLOSED, NOT IMPLEMENTED, and the disclosure says why -- the module is handed a visit and cannot know the setting, so silently filtering on a field it never receives would be worse than naming the gap. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-3-pathways-model.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- TEXAS PAS CLOSED, WISCONSIN ADDED, AND THE PATHWAYS MODEL WRITTEN DOWN AS FIVE AXES. Per Michael's instruction the model is now the standard shape, not a finding about four states: A qualification route (one of N alternatives -- licence, approved program, competency evaluation, documented experience, or an hour-denominated course), B criminal-record check (authority, timing window, staleness limit, barred offences), C registry (a dated query or listing event), D health screening, E supervision/delegation. Hours, where they exist, are ONE OPTION INSIDE AXIS A and never the axis. THE TEXAS ANSWER REVERSES AN ASSUMPTION I WAS CARRYING: there is NO training-hour requirement for an unlicensed personal assistant. The 75 hours verified on 2026-08-29 belongs to HOME HEALTH AIDES under the Licensed & Certified category; a PAS attendant is qualified by employability verification plus RN-determined competency where nursing tasks are delegated. Applying 75 hours to a Texas PAS agency would impose a requirement Texas does not make -- the same state sits in different places on the same axis depending on which LICENCE CATEGORY the employer holds (26 TAC 558.13 lists seven). Also: 558.247 bars employing anyone convicted of an offence barring employment under Health & Safety Code 250.006(a)(b)(d) AND anyone currently under deferred adjudication community supervision for such an offence; RN delegation runs through 22 TAC 225 with assessment before delegation and RN responsibility for competency evaluation; a PAS agency MAY NOT fill weekly pill minders; the 8+16 clock hours I logged on 2026-08-29 are ADMINISTRATOR training (558.244(a)(3)), not an attendant requirement, and a PAS-only administrator need not be licensed; a virtual office is not permitted and an administrative support site may not schedule visits or complete clinical documentation, which bites a franchise model. Source is HHSC's own HCSSA FAQ dated JULY 2026 -- current, with <Added 07/02/26> markers -- so it is an agency restating its own rule, one step from the codified 26 TAC text, and it is labelled that way rather than promoted. WISCONSIN GIVES AXIS C ITS SECOND POLARITY and this is why a boolean will not do: DHS 133.02(4) defines a home health aide as an individual WHOSE NAME IS ON THE REGISTRY and who is eligible for employment, under RN supervision -- registry presence as the QUALIFICATION. Massachusetts queries a registry for ADVERSE FINDINGS and is forbidden to hire on a hit. Same axis, opposite polarity; a single registry_checked flag cannot distinguish them. Caregiver takes its meaning from Wis. Stat. 50.065(1)(ag), so axis B arrives by cross-reference. TALLY: of eighteen states now read, FOUR name an hour figure (WA 75+5, TX HHA-only 75, CA 5+5, GA 40 as one of four routes) -- training_hours would be null for fourteen. TWO NEGATIVE FINDINGS RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT REDISCOVERED: the header-set fetch is NOT universal -- it unblocked mass.gov, medicaid.gov and hhs.texas.gov but Arizona (apps.azsos.gov 403), Tennessee (publications.tnsosfiles.com 403), Maryland (dsd.maryland.gov 404) and the Seattle DWO URL (404) still refuse, so reporting it as THE fix would repeat the same over-generalisation as calling the original block a request fingerprint. And Missouri 19 CSR 30-26 fetched cleanly at 189 KB and contains nothing relevant -- a guessed citation that returns HTTP 200 is still a wrong citation, logged so nobody re-fetches it. Ohio 3701-60-04 and Colorado 6 CCR 1011-1 are fetched and on disk but NOT yet read. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-4-oh-co-md-az-tn.md (NEW), docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-3-pathways-model.md (Fourth) -- FIVE STATES ADDED, THREE BLOCKED PUBLISHERS OPENED, AND TWO OF MY OWN CLAIMS CORRECTED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS ADDED. CORRECTION 1: round 3 logged Ohio and Colorado as "FETCHED, NOT YET READ". Neither was on disk -- the probe loop wrote every body to /dev/null, so "fetched" rested on a status code and nothing else, the same error as the Missouri citation logged in the same table. And the Colorado URL was not 6 CCR 1011-1 at all; it resolves to 10 CCR 2505-10 s8.500, the Medicaid BENEFIT rule. HTTP 200 is not possession and it is not relevance. CORRECTION 2: "Tennessee 0720-11" was a guessed citation and is wrong -- 0720-11 is Certificate of Need General Criteria; home care organizations are 0720-27. Second wrong guessed citation in two days, both returning real documents about something else, which is why the fetch succeeding hides it. ROUTES: Tennessee was NEVER BLOCKED -- publications.tnsosfiles.com returns 403 application/xml, which is S3 AccessDenied on a NON-EXISTENT KEY, not a bot wall; the index at /rules/0720/0720.htm fetched fine all along and its hrefs are FLAT and date-stamped (0720-27.20260318.pdf directly under /rules/0720/), not in per-chapter subdirectories as I assumed. Correct path, instant 200, 384 KB. Maryland's canonical host is regs.maryland.gov, found by following the redirect from dsd.maryland.gov rather than settling for the elaws mirror. Arizona statutes are on azleg.gov/viewdocument. ARIZONA'S ADMINISTRATIVE CODE REMAINS CLOSED -- apps.azsos.gov and azsos.gov 403 every variant including the full header set, a Referer, and with/without .pdf; administrativerules.az.gov does not resolve. So the four-header fix did NOT generalise, exactly as flagged, and Arizona is statute-only. OHIO IS THE FIRST STATE WITH NO TRAINING RULE AT ALL: chapter 3701-60's eleven rules are definitions, applicability, licensure, enforcement, database review, criminal records check, conditional employment, disqualifying offenses, exclusionary periods/certificates/pardons, records, liability. Axis A is absent; the workforce is regulated through axis B alone. Ohio licenses BOTH skilled AND nonmedical home health services ($250 for the nonmedical application), the latter meaning home health aide + personal care services. 3701-60-06 makes the criminal records check RECURRING -- every five years, with the anniversary rule differing for pre/post 2008-01-01 hires -- and 3701-60-07 conditional employment needs three things together (clean database review, fingerprint sheet BEFORE start, records check requested within five business days after). 3701-60-08 bars employing or CONTINUING to employ anyone convicted, pleading guilty, or found eligible for intervention in lieu of conviction; 3701-60-09 then supplies exclusionary periods, certificates and pardons, so a disqualification can EXPIRE OR BE CURED and a boolean background_check_passed cannot represent it. COLORADO runs axis A through the Nurse Aide Practice Act (CNA), with an explicit axis-E record-keeping duty: where a CNA performs skilled tasks requiring competency or delegation the agency must have a competency-determination process and retain all testing documentation in the personnel file. Licensure chapter still unlocated. MARYLAND (COMAR 10.07.05 Residential Service Agencies) puts axes B and D in one enumerated pre-REFERRAL list -- criminal history or private background check, licensure verification, TB screening, references, employment history, I-9, identity/eligibility, an IN-PERSON INTERVIEW, and a SKILLS ASSESSMENT AND DEMONSTRATION, the last two of which a background_check field would lose entirely. .10D-E bars referring a non-certified caregiver unless the client needs no ADL help, or the supervising nurse judges no predictable adverse consequences, or the client SIGNS A WAIVER OF SKILLED SERVICES. .11 training is topics not hours, external training needs WRITTEN OHCQ APPROVAL, and it is the first state in this survey to name CPR. ARIZONA A.R.S. 36-411 gives axis C a THIRD polarity: fingerprint clearance card or apply within twenty working days, plus -- beginning 2025-01-01 -- verify a potential employee is not on the adult protective services registry and MAY NOT HIRE if they are, AND on or before 2025-03-31 verify EVERY EXISTING EMPLOYEE. A model holding one registry event per worker cannot express a one-off dated sweep of the whole existing workforce. TENNESSEE 0720-27 defines a home health aide as 75 hours including 16 clinical "prior to OR DURING the first three months of employment" -- same number as Washington, different gate: WA runs to 120 days with 5 hours BEFORE any care, TN names no pre-service floor, so a model storing "75 + a deadline" gets Washington wrong. TN also has its own statewide abuse registry (0720-39) with UAPA contested-case due process and removal petitions, so like MA registry status is a DATED STATE, not a permanent flag -- but whether it imposes a pre-hire QUERY duty on the employer was NOT established and symmetry with MA is explicitly not assumed. TALLY: 23 states, FIVE name an hour figure; training_hours would be null for eighteen and Ohio would be null on the whole axis. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-5-tn-co-full.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- TENNESSEE AND OHIO TURN OUT TO BE EXACT COMPLEMENTS, COLORADO'S ROLES STACK, AND ARIZONA'S ADMIN CODE IS CLOSED WITH THE ATTEMPTS LISTED. TN 0720-27 read in full (107 KB, version 2026-03-18): a FULL-TEXT SWEEP FINDS NO CRIMINAL-BACKGROUND-CHECK REQUIREMENT anywhere in the chapter -- every "criminal" hit is liability language in the health-care-decisions rule. That is the mirror image of Ohio, whose entire chapter is background checks with no training rule at all. Either state alone would have suggested the wrong universal shape; the pair is what makes the model honest, and it is worth deliberately seeking the state shaped LEAST like the ones already read. TN's 75/16 IS NOT A STATE NUMBER: .06(7)(a) says "Any home health aide training programs must comply with the federal home health aide training and competency regulations" -- a POINTER to the federal standard, the same coupling shape CT and OR have on overtime. Store the pointer, not the integer. TN also gives axis E a schedulable per-patient obligation nobody else has specified: .06(7)(c) requires an RN supervisory visit to the PATIENT'S RESIDENCE at least MONTHLY, alternating between aide-present and aide-absent, to assess competence -- that belongs in a scheduling product, not a compliance sidebar. Plus RN assigns the aide to a particular patient with written instructions, annual influenza vaccination offered to all staff, and infection-control continuing education evidenced by ability to verbalise or demonstrate. TWO TN NEGATIVES SCOPED, NOT OVERSTATED: whether a background-check duty sits elsewhere in T.C.A. tit. 68 was NOT established, and 0720-39 governs Commission procedure for placing/removing people on the vulnerable-persons registry -- whether it obliges an EMPLOYER to query before hiring, as MA 155.010(E)(3) does, was NOT established and symmetry is explicitly not assumed. COLORADO 6 CCR 1011-1 CHAPTER 26 (adopted 2025-04-16, effective 2025-07-01, ruleVersionId 12016) is the richest state so far and breaks two schema assumptions. ROLES STACK: 7.4 says a personal care worker "must meet ALL requirements in Part 7.3, Homemaker, IN ADDITION TO" its own, so PCW is a SUPERSET, not a sibling row -- storing one requirements record per role duplicates the homemaker list and the two drift apart the next time 7.3 is amended. The model needs role inheritance. And some requirements are PER-ASSIGNMENT not per-role: 7.4(C)(1)(e) requires demonstrated ability with the SPECIFIC adaptive equipment that worker will encounter. AXIS B HAS TWO INCOMPATIBLE SHAPES: OH/TX/VA/FL enumerate barred offences; COLORADO DELEGATES THE JUDGMENT TO THE AGENCY -- a check for conviction of a felony or misdemeanour "which involves conduct that the agency determines could pose a risk", Colorado search, not more than 90 days before employment, plus a required policy. A barred_offences[] field has nothing to put in it for Colorado; what Colorado requires is a documented decision. COLORADO ALSO REQUIRES THREE SEPARATE PRE-EMPLOYMENT LOOKUPS, each with its own authority and record: criminal history, the CAPS Check (Colorado Adult Protective Services Data System, C.R.S. 26-3.1-111), and DORA verification that any licence/registration/certification exists and is IN GOOD STANDING with a copy of the inquiry filed. With Arizona that is now two of twenty-four states running an APS-registry check as a hard pre-hire gate. Colorado is also the third two-category licence (Part 6 Skilled, Part 7 Non-Medical/Personal Care) after Ohio and Texas, and its recurring training is counted in TOPICS not hours -- at least four of the 7.3(C)(1) topics every twelve months. AXIS A NOW HAS SIX DISTINCT WRITTEN FORMS plus registry-presence (WI) and none-at-all (OH). ARIZONA CLOSED WITH THE LEDGER SHOWN rather than left as an open intention: bare curl, UA-only curl, full header set, full headers plus Referer, azsos.gov and www.azsos.gov variants, administrativerules.az.gov (does not resolve), Chrome (loads it -- canvas viewer, no text), fetch() from the PDF tab (returned empty), fetch() from an HTML page on the same origin (CDP Runtime.evaluate TIMED OUT after 45 s). Eight approaches across two sessions; stopped per the rabbit-hole rule. Arizona is statutory only. A.R.S. 36-425.01 and 36-144 are identified and NOT fetched but sit on azleg.gov which works, so they are the cheap next step. METHOD: the Colorado CCR rule-info page exposes NO href to the document -- the id lives in an inline onclick, OpenRuleWindow('12016', ...) -- so an href-only extractor reports a dead end on a page that is not one. Grep the raw HTML for the JS call. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-6-az-co-placement-sc.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- ARIZONA'S BLOCKED ADMIN CODE MATTERED LESS THAN I RECORDED, AND COLORADO CONTAINS THE FIRST RULE A PRODUCT MUST IMPLEMENT BY WITHHOLDING A FEATURE. Round 5 closed AAC R9-10 after eight approaches and called Arizona "statutory only", implying a large gap. Reading the two remaining statutes changes that: for the NON-MEDICAL segment the statute IS the regime. A.R.S. 36-144 requires a business providing home care services to disclose ANNUALLY TO EACH CLIENT whether criminal background checks are performed AND its policy on sending people with a criminal history into a home; the name and position of whoever manages, hires, fires and terminates contracts; a description of any required training AND WHETHER IT INCLUDES FIRST AID AND CPR; the services and the cost of each; and the agreement terms and how to terminate. 36-144(B)(2) EXCLUDES title 32/36 licensees, which is exactly what makes it apply to the unlicensed operators who have no other compliance scaffolding -- reading the exclusion is what turned this from marginal into the most product-relevant part of Arizona's scheme. SEVENTH AXIS-A SHAPE AND IT IS THE INVERSE OF ALL THE OTHERS: no training standard at all, but a mandatory annual per-client written disclosure OF WHAT YOUR STANDARD IS. Maryland mandates CPR; Arizona mandates disclosing whether you mandate CPR. The compliance artefact is a DOCUMENT generated per client per year from the agency's own configuration, which "does this worker qualify?" cannot produce. 36-425.01 adds a DEEMED-STATUS route: in lieu of a state licensure survey the department SHALL license a home health agency that is Medicare certified and has been department-licensed at least a year -- so an Arizona agency's posture can be driven by CMS cycles rather than state inspection. COLORADO PART 3 PLACEMENT AGENCIES: separate registration, unregistered operation is a MISDEMEANOUR plus up to $10,000 per violation. 3.2 uses the same agency-judgment criminal-history standard as Part 5 but adds FIVE ENUMERATED FACTORS the agency SHALL consider -- history of convictions/pleas, nature and seriousness, TIME ELAPSED, MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES, and the NATURE OF THE POSITION -- so it is a structured, position-specific, documented judgment, not a pass/fail gate, and a boolean loses the entire rule. 3.3 IS A HARD PRODUCT CONSTRAINT: a written disclosure in the Department's prescribed form, SIGNED BY THE CONSUMER BEFORE SERVICES START, stating the agency is not the employer and "does not direct, control, SCHEDULE, or train any provider it refers". Scheduling is named. For that customer segment scheduling, training delivery and assignment-direction are not features to build carefully -- they must NOT BE OFFERED, and the product has to know which registration a customer holds before exposing them. First rule in this survey implemented by WITHHOLDING a feature. Also 3.4 seven-year record retention and weekday 9-5 inspections covering criminal history, GENERAL LIABILITY INSURANCE and disclosures; 3.6 intermediate restrictions incl. a ten-calendar-day written plan and $10,000/year fines. SO A REGISTRATION-CATEGORY DIMENSION SITS ABOVE THE FIVE AXES: a licensed Colorado HCA must train, evaluate, supervise and schedule while a registered placement agency must not -- same chapter, opposite duties. FL registration-vs-licensure, OH skilled-vs-nonmedical, TX's seven categories and CO Part 6/Part 7 are all the same thing: the category decides which axes apply at all. SOUTH CAROLINA Title 44 Ch. 69 read (Licensure of Home Health Agencies Act, 1978 Act No. 548): definitions only, NO training, competency or background requirement -- statute enables, regulation specifies, and DHEC R.61-77 was NOT located, so SC's axes A/B/D are unanswered. ROUTES: scstatehouse.gov/code works, /coderegs index loads with no findable link pattern; iga.in.gov returns a 691-byte SPA shell on every path but api.iga.in.gov answers in JSON with "x-api-key not found" and points at docs.api.iga.in.gov -- INDIANA IS NOT BLOCKED, IT IS ASKING FOR A CREDENTIAL, which is a decision to make rather than an obstacle, and filing it under "blocked" would have hidden an available option; rules.ok.gov 403 on chapter and title; doa.la.gov LAC Title 48 404 on every path tried. METHOD: closing a SOURCE is not closing a QUESTION -- ask what the blocked source was for before recording the gap at full width, because round 5 recorded Arizona as a bigger hole than it actually is. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-7-south-carolina.md (NEW), round-6 and round-3 docs edited (Fourth) -- R.61-77 DOES NOT EXIST ANY MORE, AND THE 404 WAS THE ANSWER. Round 6 recorded "DHEC R.61-77 was not located" as a routing failure. It was not: 2023 Act No. 60 (S.399) ABOLISHED DHEC effective 2024-07-01, and the regulation is now 60-77 in Chapter 60 (Department of Public Health), "Transferred from 61-75 and amended by SCSR 49-5 Doc. No. 5352, eff May 23, 2025". Chapter 61.pdf 404s on the publisher's own server while Chapter 60.pdf returns 1.99 MB / 787 pages. A citation is a claim with a date on it -- same failure family as the wrong-guess Missouri and Tennessee citations, inverted: there the URL resolved to the wrong document, here the right document refused to resolve under a lapsed number. Cost a full round; the lesson is to check whether the citation still exists before concluding the publisher is awkward. SOUTH CAROLINA MAPPED: 501(B) DELEGATES AXIS A TO THE AGENCY IN WRITING -- the agency "shall define in writing the responsibilities, qualifications, and competencies of Staff for all positions", and Section 500 is titled "Staff and Training" yet contains only General, Administrator, Clinical Manager and Health Status, with NO aide training rule and NO hour figure anywhere. That makes SC and Colorado exact opposites in method: Colorado delegates AXIS B (the barred-offence judgment) and specifies axis A in detail; South Carolina enumerates barred offences and delegates axis A. Same delegation device, opposite axes -- which is why delegation has to be modelled as a SHAPE, not as a property of one axis. AXIS B THIRD SHAPE: 501(A) requires a check under S.C. Code 44-7-2910 and bars conviction or nolo plea for unlawful conduct toward a child (63-45-70), abuse/neglect/exploitation of a vulnerable adult (43-35-10 et seq.), "OR ANY SIMILAR CRIMINAL OFFENSE" -- enumerated then delegated at the edge, and it binds VOLUNTEERS, which most states' wording does not. A barred_offences[] list holds the first two and cannot hold the third, which is the one needing a human decision. "Similar offense" and Colorado's "the agency determines" are the same instruction reached from opposite directions: both need somewhere to record REASONING, not a flag. AXIS D: 504 requires a documented Health Assessment within TWELVE MONTHS PRIOR TO INITIAL PATIENT CONTACT -- anchored to first contact, not to hire -- including tuberculin testing per 1702, and copies must be accessible AT EACH LOCATION where a staff member works for the same Licensee. Neither is a boolean or a single date. Also 501(C) records date of hire and date of initial patient contact as SEPARATE fields; 502 full-time Administrator with the same three-route test as TN and TX; 601 incidents retained SIX YEARS after last discharge. COLORADO SCHEDULING PROHIBITION PROMOTED PER INSTRUCTION: round 6 now opens with a boxed BUILD CONSTRAINT before any other content, and the pathways-model doc carries the same warning ABOVE the five-axis table so anyone reading the model first cannot miss it. The framing recorded in both: every other registration-category instance changes WHICH REQUIREMENTS APPLY; only this one changes WHAT THE SOFTWARE IS ALLOWED TO DO -- account-level capability gate, keyed on registration category, decided before UI is exposed, and hidden-but-enableable is the same exposure. INDIANA NEEDS MICHAEL: the API is public and documented and would return IC 16-27 and IAC 410 as JSON, but getting the x-api-key requires REGISTERING AN ACCOUNT with the Indiana General Assembly, which I do not do. Endpoint shape already confirmed: https://api.iga.in.gov/{year}/code/title/{n}/article/{n}. OKLAHOMA AND LOUISIANA STILL UNRESOLVED, recorded with the routes tried rather than as pending intentions: OK -- rules.ok.gov 403s chapter, title and document paths, oklahoma.gov and sos.ok.gov 404, oscn.net responds but is the courts network; LA -- doa.la.gov (Office of the State Register) 200s at root and 404s on five LAC paths, its /media/ slugs are opaque so guessing cannot work, ldh.la.gov is programme info not rules, while legis.la.gov DOES serve statutes so R.S. 40:2009.31 et seq. is reachable and unfetched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-8-oklahoma-louisiana.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- BOTH STATES OPENED ON THE FIRST TRY ONCE I STOPPED GUESSING PATHS. Round 7 recorded OK and LA as "no route found" after four and five attempts respectively; both fell immediately to the South Carolina method -- fetch the publisher's OWN INDEX and read its links. OKLAHOMA: rules.ok.gov still 403s everything, but the statutes are on oscn.net, WHICH I HAD ALREADY FETCHED IN ROUND 6 AND DISMISSED AS "the courts network". It is the courts network AND it publishes the Oklahoma Statutes; index.asp?ftdb=STOKST63&level=1 returns 634 KB of Title 63 and sections come from DeliverDocument.asp?CiteID=<n>. A host's BRANDING is not its CONTENTS. LOUISIANA: one fetch of doa.la.gov's root plus a grep of its links for "administrative code" gave /doa/osr/louisiana-administrative-code/; Title 48 is published as TWO .docx FILES at opaque /media/<slug>/ paths, which is exactly why slug-guessing could never work. .docx means pypdf is the wrong tool -- zipfile + word/document.xml + tag-strip yields 3.67M and 3.36M chars of clean text, worth keeping alongside the PDF path. FIVE STATES NOW SOLVED BY FETCHING THE INDEX AND EVERY PATH I INVENTED FROM MEMORY FAILED; that is now the default move, not a fallback. OKLAHOMA (63 O.S. 1-1960 et seq., Home Care Act): 1-1962(C)(1) bars employing or contracting anyone as a home health aide FOR MORE THAN FOUR MONTHS unless they are a licensed health professional or have satisfied certification AND PLACEMENT ON THE STATE HOME HEALTH AIDE REGISTRY -- so the REGISTRY AXIS CAN CARRY A DEADLINE, a third timing shape after WA (120 days with a 5-hour pre-service floor) and TN (three months, no floor). 1-1961(2) makes "certification" cover home health aides AND agency administrators through one mechanism. The grandfathering clause leaves a live per-task rule: the agency "shall only assign the home health aide to tasks for which the aide has been determined to be competent" -- per-assignment, not per-credential, same shape as Colorado's adaptive-equipment clause. 1-1962(B) IS A MARKET-STRUCTURE RULE WITH NO ANALOGUE ELSEWHERE HERE: no licensed health care facility, physician, APRN, PA or state agency employee may REFER a client for personal care or companion/sitter services except to a licensed agency, with "facility" defined to include assisted living, adult day care and hospice -- every other state constrains who may WORK; Oklahoma also constrains WHO MAY BE SENT BUSINESS, so any referral or partner-network feature needs its own licensure check. AND EXEMPTIONS ARE PER-AXIS, NOT PER-ENTITY: DHS personal-care contractors are excluded from "home care agency" but the statute says expressly they "shall not be exempt from certification as home health aides". A boolean exempt flag is wrong. Also excluded: CD-PASS ISOs, CD-PASS employer participants, and PACE organizations. LOUISIANA (LAC 48:I ch. 91) GIVES THE CLEAREST UNLICENSED-SEGMENT STATEMENT IN THE SURVEY: nothing in the Part prohibits delivery of personal care, homemaker, respite and other in-home services by an entity NOT LICENSED under the Rule "unless provided with other home health services" -- Louisiana is the null case and the trigger that flips it is BUNDLING. Its home health aide definition embeds a product requirement: qualified to provide direct care under RN or PT supervision, per a written plan of care, "and requiring A CLINICAL NOTE FOR EACH PATIENT VISIT" -- the second behaviour constraint found, and the opposite polarity to Colorado's: Colorado FORBIDS a feature, Louisiana COMPELS one. Axis C sits on the administrator, who must assure policies are enforced "including but not limited to checking the direct service worker (DSW)/certified nurse aide (CNA) registry for adverse actions against non-licensed employees". LA also bars an HHA from sharing office space with a non-healthcare-related entity and requires it be a separate entity from any other business. NOT READ AND FLAGGED: OAC 310:661 (still 403, and OK 1-1963/1-1964 delegate training hours, competency testing and registry mechanics to exactly those rules -- so the fully-read statute tells me the SHAPE of Oklahoma's answer, certification plus registry, but not its content), OK 1-1972, LA ch. 92 Direct Service Worker Registry including 9245 training requirements and 9247 annual competency evaluation, and LA 9103 Personnel Qualifications. 27 states on at least one axis. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-9-la-ch92-missouri-kentucky.md (NEW), rounds 3 and 4 corrected (Fourth) -- MISSOURI WAS NOT A WRONG CITATION AND I SAID IT WAS, THREE ROUNDS RUNNING. Round 3 logged 19 CSR 30-26 as "WRONG CHAPTER, ABANDONED... the citation was a guess and it was wrong", and rounds 4 and 5 carried it. The Secretary of State's own Title 19 index lists 19c30-26.pdf as "Chapter 26 - Home Health Agencies". THE CITATION WAS RIGHT; MY SCAN WAS TOO NARROW -- I searched only for hour patterns, found none, and reported "nothing relevant", which is a DIFFERENT CLAIM AND ONE I NEVER TESTED. Corrected in three places: the round-3 Tier 2 row, the round-3 method note (which had generalised a false negative into "a guessed citation that fetches cleanly is still a wrong citation" -- that lesson is real but its true example is Tennessee 0720-11, and the note now says so), and the round-4 carry-forward row. The surviving lesson is narrower and better: a negative from a single pattern is a negative ABOUT THAT PATTERN. WHAT MISSOURI ACTUALLY SAYS: 19 CSR 30-26.010 INCORPORATES 42 CFR 484 WHOLESALE -- agencies "shall strictly meet the currently applicable Medicare Conditions of Participation" and state licensure surveys are conducted PER MEDICARE STANDARDS. Purest federal-coupling instance in the survey: TN's hours point at the federal competency rule, CT and OR define overtime coverage by FLSA reference, Missouri adopts an entire federal chapter INCLUDING THE SURVEY METHODOLOGY, and "currently applicable" means it moves with no Missouri rulemaking. On top of that, dementia-specific training for employees AND INDEPENDENT CONTRACTORS who provide direct care or MAY HAVE DAILY CONTACT with affected clients, five minimum topics, no hour figure. KENTUCKY (902 KAR 20:081) IS A FULL FIVE-AXIS STATE and its necessity clause names its own driver (KRS 216.9375(11)(a), dementia training), which is how the overlay was found before reading the body. Dementia care: SIX HOURS INITIAL PLUS THREE HOURS ANNUAL, and the initial training must be completed BEFORE providing services to a patient exhibiting symptoms -- so THE PATIENT'S CONDITION IS A TRIGGER FOR AXIS A, not the worker's role or the task. With Missouri (topics, no hours) and California (a dementia topic added to the annual five from 2027-01-01) that is three states, three mechanisms, one subject: a dementia overlay is now its own dimension. KY supervision has TWO CADENCES SELECTED BY SERVICE MIX -- every two weeks by the responsible practitioner (aide present to observe and assist, or absent to assess relationships), but every SIXTY days by an RN where the patient receives no skilled service, and that visit must occur WHILE THE AIDE IS PROVIDING CARE. A single supervisory_visit_interval_days field cannot hold it. KY also runs axes B and C at full strength: pre-employment abuse registry checks under KRS 216.937 and 209.032 AND pre-employment criminal checks barring felony theft, drug abuse/possession/sale, abuse/neglect/exploitation of a child or adult, sexual crime, or misdemeanour abuse/neglect/exploitation of an adult. LOUISIANA CH. 92 CONTAINS THE MOST PRODUCT-RELEVANT RULE FOUND SO FAR. 9245(A): person-specific training from an RN WHO HAS ASSESSED THE HEALTH STATUS OF THE PERSON and determined the worker can perform the tasks safely FOR THIS PERSON; the determination is certified in writing, kept in the personnel file, and SHALL NOT BE DELEGATED; repeated if not certified; and refreshed when THE PERSON'S health status or physician orders change. Competency is therefore a TRIPLE -- worker x client x task set -- not a property of the worker, so a caregiver authorised for Client A is NOT authorised for Client B, SUBSTITUTION IS A REGULATED ACT, the record depends on clinical events rather than a date, and it must name the certifying RN. Also 9245(B) SIXTEEN HOURS of medication administration training approved by an RN (a sixth hour figure, and TASK-scoped not role-scoped), 9247 annual RN competency evaluation, 9249 authorised tasks limited to stable persons with predictable results plus medication container labelling, and 9103 an administrator qualification that DEPENDS ON HIRE DATE (bachelor's if employed on/after 2018-01-13, three extra years if before) with any multi-agency administrator needing a full-time on-site alternate at each. PATHWAYS-MODEL DOC NOW CARRIES A BEHAVIOUR-CONSTRAINTS TABLE per Michael's instruction to keep the pattern visible: five rules, both polarities -- Colorado FORBIDS scheduling, Louisiana COMPELS a clinical note per visit and per-client RN certification, Oklahoma compels per-task assignment checking, Kentucky compels pre-service dementia training and scheduled supervisory visits -- with the note that THREE OF THE FIVE ARE TRIGGERED BY THE CLIENT OR THE TASK, NOT THE WORKER, so a model asking only "is this caregiver qualified?" answers the wrong question, and they must be checked at ASSIGNMENT time rather than onboarding. NOT DONE: MI/AL/MS/AR (two working indexes identified -- michigan.gov/lara and alabamapublichealth.gov/providerstandards; sos.ms.gov and sos.arkansas.gov 404, alabamaadministrativecode.state.al.us does not resolve), LA ch.92 subchapters A-C, KY's cross-referenced KRS sections. 29 states. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-10-arkansas-michigan.md (NEW), pathways-model doc edited (Fourth) -- ARKANSAS TRAINS THE UNLICENSED SEGMENT, MICHIGAN APPEARS NOT TO TOUCH IT, AND A 200 THAT IS REALLY A 404 NEARLY GOT LOGGED AS THREE WORKING ROUTES. LOUISIANA TRIPLE PROMOTED per instruction: the pathways-model doc now opens with a boxed section titled "Is this caregiver qualified?" is the WRONG QUESTION in at least four states -- LAC 48:I 9245(A) certifies competency for a NAMED individual, by an RN who assessed THAT person, non-delegably, refreshed on a change in that person's condition or orders, so qualification is a worker x client x task triple with no worker-level state to check. Spelled out for scheduling: a caregiver cleared for Client A is NOT cleared for Client B; open-shift claiming, auto-assignment, pool substitution and last-minute cover all need a per-client authorisation lookup BEFORE the assignment stands; the authorisation expires on a CLINICAL EVENT not a date, coupling the client record to the caregiver's authorisation; and the certifying RN must be named. Same family: OK per-task assignment, CO per-adaptive-device, KY pre-service dementia training. ARKANSAS (20 CAR Part 45, Act 1410 of 2013, eff. 2024-09-05, from codeofarrules.arkansas.gov which is the OFFICIAL code under Ark. Code 25-15-218): THE SCOPE TRIGGER IS THE CLIENT'S AGE -- caregiver services means ADL assistance where "the recipient of services is fifty (50) years of age or older AT THE TIME THE SERVICES ARE PROVIDED", the only age-scoped trigger in the survey and one a client can cross into mid-engagement. The regime attaches BY DEFINITION to the unlicensed agency: an "in-home services agency" provides caregiver services for pay in a residence AND "is not otherwise licensed by the Department of Health as a home health agency, private care agency, or hospice agency". So there are now four distinct answers to the unlicensed-segment question: LA leaves it alone unless bundled, FL registers it, AZ makes it disclose, ARKANSAS TRAINS IT -- 40 hours with no licence at all. NESTED HOUR STRUCTURE, first in the survey: 40 total, of which AT LEAST 16 must cover "physical skills and competent demonstration of such skills" across twelve enumerated skills, and AT LEAST 4 must cover Alzheimer's/dementia with four named subtopics. A single training_hours: 40 loses both inner constraints and the 16 are demonstration, not instruction. 45-103 lets the EMPLOYER CERTIFY the training if it keeps records of person, topic and TIME SPENT -- the clearest case yet where the compliance artefact and the product's own training-module data are the same thing. 45-104 exemptions include one year of institutional experience VERIFIED BY THE PRIOR EMPLOYER, and a full carve-out for CNAs/LPNs/RNs/physicians/licensed social workers, close family, uncompensated providers, court-appointed guardians, and DIRECT-CARE WORKERS SERVING A DHS-LICENSED PROGRAMME PARTICIPANT -- so an Arkansas agency's obligations differ PER CLIENT, BY PAYER, within one workforce. Axis B arrives by cross-reference to the long-term-care felony bar (20-38-101) with an expungement/pardon escape like Ohio's. DEMENTIA IS NOW A FOUR-STATE OVERLAY WITH FOUR MECHANISMS: CA one topic added to the annual five from 2027-01-01, KY 6 initial + 3 annual before serving a symptomatic patient, MO topics with no hours reaching independent contractors, AR 4 hours inside the 40. MICHIGAN: LARA's Bureau of Community and Health Systems describes its own licensing remit as adult foster care, adult camps, freestanding surgical outpatient, homes for the aged, hospice, hospitals, nursing homes, SUD programmes, certified nurse aides and medication aides -- NO home health agency, NO home care agency. WRITTEN UP WITH THE LIMIT ON IT: what is verified is the bureau's self-description, which is CONSISTENT WITH but not proof of the reported position that Michigan licenses neither; the Public Health Code (Act 368 of 1978) is NOT read, so Michigan sits in Tier 2 until it is. If it holds, Michigan is the largest state in the survey with no licensure axis at all. ALABAMA IS THE DANGEROUS ONE: alabamapublichealth.gov returns HTTP 200 WITH A "404page" BODY for every guessed path -- three different paths, byte-identical 6,109-byte 200s. A status-code-only probe would have logged three working Alabama routes; same class as the round-3 /dev/null probe arriving by a different door. CHECK THE BODY, NOT THE CODE. admincode.legislature.state.al.us is a 902-byte SPA shell. Mississippi's SoS admin-code page is navigation only and the document endpoint was not found. METHOD ALSO: Arkansas's rule links were wrapped in gcc02.safelinks.protection.outlook.com redirects with the real URL in the url= parameter, and codeofarrules renders part and subpart pages as METADATA ONLY -- the text lives at levelType=section with a sectionID, so the part page looks empty and is not. 31 states touched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-11-michigan-confirmed.md (NEW), round-10 doc corrected (Fourth) -- MICHIGAN CONFIRMED AT THE STATUTE, AND "NULL CASE" WOULD HAVE BEEN THE WRONG ANSWER REACHED FROM CORRECT EVIDENCE. Read Public Health Code 1978 PA 368 Article 17 (226 pages, complete through PA 91 of 2026) from legislature.mi.gov. MCL 333.20106(1) enumerates "health facility or agency" in ELEVEN items -- ambulance/transport services, county medical care facility, freestanding surgical outpatient, HMO, home for the aged, hospital, nursing home, hospice, hospice residence, educational-institution variants, freestanding birth center -- and HOME HEALTH AGENCY IS NOT AMONG THEM, nor home care, in-home services, personal care or homemaker services. So the round-10 negative holds and now rests on the enumerated definition rather than on a bureau's self-description. BUT READING IT TURNED UP THE HALF THAT CHANGES MICHIGAN'S CLASSIFICATION: MCL 333.20173a defines "home health agency" as A PERSON CERTIFIED BY MEDICARE providing in-residence nursing, therapeutic, social work, HOMEMAKER or home health aide services, and makes such an agency a COVERED FACILITY under a statutory employment bar. Michigan uses MEDICARE CERTIFICATION AS THE HOOK WHERE OTHER STATES USE A LICENCE -- same coupling family as MO (adopts 42 CFR 484 wholesale), TN (aide hours point at the federal rule) and CT/OR (FLSA-defined overtime coverage), except here the federal instrument decides WHO THE STATE RULE APPLIES TO AT ALL. The bar: a covered facility "shall not employ, INDEPENDENTLY CONTRACT WITH, OR GRANT CLINICAL PRIVILEGES TO" anyone who REGULARLY HAS DIRECT ACCESS TO OR PROVIDES DIRECT SERVICES TO patients, if convicted of a relevant crime under 42 USC 1320a-7(a) or of enumerated violence/death felonies (or an attempt, conspiracy, or similar state/federal crime) UNLESS FIFTEEN YEARS HAVE LAPSED SINCE COMPLETING ALL TERMS of sentencing, parole AND probation, measured at the date of application or contract execution. Three modelling points: it reaches independent contractors and clinical privileges, the widest personnel scope in the survey; "direct access" is defined to include access to a patient's PROPERTY, FINANCIAL INFORMATION, MEDICAL RECORDS, TREATMENT INFORMATION or any other identifying information, so an office worker with records access is inside the bar; and the 15-year clock is a COMPUTED DATE FROM THREE INPUTS evaluated at a fourth, not a stored flag. Also criminal penalties for false information, for misuse of criminal-history data, and FOR FAILING TO CONDUCT THE CHECKS, plus fingerprint retention and an electronic web-based notification system (rap-back), with 20173b giving a disqualified individual an APPEAL, so disqualification is contestable like the MA and TN registry-removal petitions. MICHIGAN IS OHIO'S AXIS PROFILE REACHED BY THE OPPOSITE ROUTE: Ohio licenses both skilled and nonmedical home health and its chapter is nothing but background checks; Michigan does not license at all and still imposes the regime, by hanging it on Medicare certification. Two states, same profile, incompatible mechanisms -- neither a "licensed?" nor a "regulated?" flag distinguishes them. FLAGGED AS INFERENCE, NOT VERIFIED: a Michigan NON-MEDICAL in-home care business is neither licensed nor Medicare-certified and so appears to fall outside both definitions, but that follows from two definitions and Michigan's other acts were not searched. Also noted: MCL 333.20173 itself was REPEALED by 2006 PA 28 -- its repeal note is what identified the live sections, so a repealed section still answers questions. ALABAMA AND MISSISSIPPI: stopped at NINE attempts across the two states, consistent with the Arizona precedent, and recorded the ledger. BOTH STATES ROUTE THEIR OWN CODES TO LEXISNEXIS and both link to it from their own sites -- that IS the designated route and it is JavaScript-gated, so the state has effectively no fetchable primary source, which is a property of the state rather than a failure of the attempt. alison and admincode are SPA shells (1.9 KB and 902 bytes), alabamapublichealth.gov serves byte-identical 200s with a "404page" body, alisondb fails to connect, lexisnexis mscode renders nothing without JS, and Justia 403s Alabama while serving a Cloudflare interstitial for Mississippi -- and Justia is secondary anyway, so even success would not have been Tier 1. A browser might render all of them, but the Arizona experience is why that is not automatically the answer, and neither state is worth that cost ahead of the ~20 states not yet attempted. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-12-nevada.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- NEVADA PUTS A WAGE OBLIGATION INSIDE A LICENSURE REGULATION, AND HAS A BRANCH WHERE THE CLIENT TRAINS THE WORKER. NAC 449 (1.6M chars from leg.state.nv.us) licenses "an agency to provide personal care services in the home" AS ITS OWN FACILITY CATEGORY -- a FIFTH distinct answer to the unlicensed-segment question after LA (leave alone unless bundled), FL (register), AZ (disclose) and AR (train without licensing). Substantive rules at NAC 449.396-449.398x with their own defined terms including "attendant" and "work station". AXIS A: 449.3975 requires attendants to be 18+, understand chapter 449, demonstrate ability to read/write/speak and communicate effectively with clients, and receive ANNUALLY NOT LESS THAN 8 HOURS of training; 449.3977 requires a working knowledge of the licensing provisions BEFORE providing services at all (and the agency must hand over a copy), then completion of a training programme INCLUDING SUPERVISED ON-THE-JOB INSTRUCTION WITH REAL CLIENTS before working independently, with the administrator or designee deciding whether the attendant can work independently. Required topics: written documentation of services AND VERIFICATION OF TIME RECORDS, client rights and confidentiality, the sensory/physical/cognitive changes of ageing, communication skills incl. alternative modes for sensory-impaired clients, and first aid + CPR. "Verification of time records" being a NAMED TRAINING TOPIC in a licensure rule is the EVV discipline arriving from the state-licensure side rather than the Medicaid side. Nevada is also the second CPR state after Maryland and goes further: 449.3976 requires a Red Cross or equivalent first-aid-and-CPR certificate ON FILE WITHIN SIX MONTHS of starting. THE FINDING THAT CHANGES PAYROLL RATHER THAN COMPLIANCE: 449.39735 makes the agency PAY THE COST OF TRAINING, THE TRAVEL COSTS, AND THE EMPLOYEE'S OWN SALARY OR HOURLY WAGE FOR BOTH TRAINING TIME AND ROUND-TRIP TRAVEL TIME, with exceptions for on-premises training and for an employee who attends but FAILS TO COMPLETE. Every other state's training requirement is a compliance fact; Nevada's is also TIMESHEET LINES THAT MUST EXIST, and it is the first place axis A and the wage/overtime research meet in a single rule. 449.3976's personnel file also shows a PER-ASSIGNMENT DOCUMENT: liability-insurance proof is required only IF THE ATTENDANT WILL PROVIDE TRANSPORT -- same conditional shape as Colorado's adaptive equipment and Oklahoma's task competency, applied to a document rather than a skill. Plus TB via NAC 441A.375, background via NRS 449.123, and reference checks evidenced. SELF-DIRECTED BRANCH INVERTS THE DATA FLOW: 449.39519 is titled "Client to serve as managing employer and PROVIDE TRAINING TO PERSONAL ASSISTANT; reporting of training to organization" -- no other state places the training obligation on the person receiving care, and for that segment the training record is SUBMITTED BY THE CLIENT rather than generated by the agency. LABELLED HONESTLY: that finding rests on the SECTION HEADING and its placement, not on its text, and the doc says so. Also NAC 449.749(3) defines home health aide as A NURSING ASSISTANT under NRS 632.0166 -- axis A by cross-reference to the Board of Nursing, the same device Colorado uses. NOT READ AND FLAGGED AS THE TWO TO TAKE NEXT: 449.3978 (attendants: PROHIBITION on providing certain services -- a negative-capability rule of the Colorado family) and 449.3979 (written disclosure statement to the client on acceptance -- a disclosure artefact of the Arizona family).
